| G-6 | **Patricia-trie links drawn** | **Done — seen live 2026-09-19** — `confirm.mjs`, re-runnable and idempotent. Unfolding RFC 9110 draws **26 nodes and 26 links**, 0 unresolved endpoints, 0 dangling; all 26 carry a three.js line object attached to the scene, and the star is visible on screen. **The `Confirm` was hiding a defect.** The row claimed 31 nodes and 30 edges; live it was 26 and **0**, because `_edges_among` cannot draw an edge at the step a reader takes: containment runs parent-to-child and the branches are disjoint subtrees, so every edge it finds lies inside one branch and is dropped as a self-edge. Measured on that page: 1,072 containment edges, 662 with both endpoints surviving, **0 crossing two branches**. The 30 edges were a star from the parent, and `unfoldNode` had started REMOVING the parent. The hub now stays, shrinks to its smallest branch, and drops its picture |
| G-7 | **Markdown retrieval links drawn** | **Confirm** 2026-10-24 live (check `G-7`): a note written into the vault with a retrieval-style link to a stored page (`Radix_tree`), indexed as the `[]()` gesture's infill now indexes it, became a page `vault://<vault>/<path>`, its link resolved at ingest, placed by the projector, and drawn in 3D as an edge to that page **9-19 s** after indexing; planted `__domvecPlantNoNoteIndex`: no page, no edge; note, page and folder removed after. Built: sidecar `POST /note` (gated as a capture; `tests/test_note_route.py` 2), the plugin indexing a note on infill and re-indexing it on every edit thereafter (`indexedNotes`), the note handed to the projector at once (it is embedded inside the ingest, so the embed worker never queued it). Found on the way: `treeOnly` kept ONE incoming edge per node for page links too, so the page view drew **232 of 914** layout edges -- a well-linked page kept one link; now the one-parent rule is for containment only (planted `plantTreeOnlyPages`), 914 of 914 drawn. Only notes the reader wrote retrieval links into are indexed, not the vault. |
| G-8 | **Module dependency links** for codebases and Python modules | **Done 2026-09-20** — and NOT as a second graph. An import is categorised as a link, so `ContentAddress.is_url` says so, the store's existing `_link_pages` resolver picks it up, `edges()` routes it and PageRank counts it — the same treatment a hyperlink gets, which is the whole reason for spending an address on it. Measured on `domvec/` itself: **102 modules, 232 dependency edges, 2,180 edges for PageRank** |
| G-9 | …including modules inside **local PyPI packages** | **Done 2026-09-20** — the same walker pointed at an installed package, located by import rather than guessed at a path. Measured on numpy: 12 files → 510 addresses, 123 chunks, addressed `code://pypi/numpy/…` |
| G-10 | **Vault directory** drawn as links | **Done 2026-09-20** — a note's links become `LinksTo` edges through the same resolver a hyperlink and an import use. One change made it possible: `urljoin` performs relative resolution only for schemes it KNOWS, so `urljoin("vault://v/a.md", "b.md")` hands back `"b.md"` unchanged — not an error, just the input — and every vault link therefore resolved to nothing. `canonical_url` joins local schemes by hand now. A wikilink gets the `.md` spelling offered as well, since `[[Lattice]]` names a note and a note is a file; the store's own rule still decides, so a wikilink to a note nobody indexed stays an address — asserted as its own arm |
| G-11 | **Three sources in one skeleton**: web, vault, PyPI | **Done 2026-09-20** — one store holding all four spellings at once: `https://`, `vault://`, `code://` for the repository and `code://pypi/` for an installed package. Measured: 59 chunks, 653 addresses, **one trie**, every chunk's xpath resolving the same way whatever it came from. `tests/test_source_corpus.py` asserts the schemes coexist |
| G-12 | **UMAP embeddings persisted to the db** and queried straight into the graph view | **Done 2026-09-20** — a `Layout` node table keyed on everything the fit is a function of (corpus size, dims, space, level), so a stored answer comes back only to the question it answered. Measured on `corpus123` across a sidecar RESTART: **19.3s → 0.15s**, and the reply says `from_store: true` so a reader can tell which they got. A table rather than columns on `Chunk`: the payload belongs to a fit, and four fits of one corpus (page/chunk × euclidean/S³) would not fit in one column each. **The hazard it introduces is guarded**: a fitted reducer is a MODEL and does not serialise, so serving a stored layout would have left every unfolding answering "no fit to place into yet". `_ensure_projection` builds one on demand — verified live, 26 nodes and 26 edges out of a process that had only ever served a stored picture |
| G-13 | Re-projection **batched in a queue whose latency adapts** to the projection's own cost | **Confirm** 2026-10-30 -- the window adapts to the ARRIVALS as well as to the cost, and is on again. The 2026-10-24 measurement was of bursts (a page's chunks arrive from the embedder at once, so waiting only added latency); a live page's mutations arrive a few at a time, and only there can waiting gather company. So the window opens only while recent drains averaged under 8 chunks (`TRICKLE`) and a woken queue holding 8 or more goes at once (`projector.trickling`). Live (`checks/live.mjs` G-13, served `busy_feed.html` and `nav_heavy.html`): on the trickle the window opened **25 times**, median wait **1.0 ms** against a drain cost of 10 ms; on the burst it opened **0 times**; planted `window-always` made the burst wait (p50 **157 ms**). The BENEFIT is reported, not scored: drains on the trickle went 62 -> 51 and 61 -> 44 in two runs (3.95 -> 4.86 and 4.03 -> 4.73 chunks a drain), and the other way in a third whose arms streamed different volumes (514 vs 188 chunks). Found on the way, and fixed: a re-cluster re-queued EVERY chunk of its page for re-projection, after every quiet gap of a live page -- O(page) where the fold is O(delta). Now only a chunk whose group changed, the leads on both sides, one not placed yet, and one placed that the page lost (`sidecar._project_regrouped`); live, one re-cluster queued **15 of the page's 81**. Planted `project-whole-page`: all of it (`tests/test_project_regrouped.py`, 3). Was: **Partly** 2026-10-24, measured and decided by the measurement. Built: the projector's queue records each chunk's wait, what a drain costs and chunks per drain (`/projector` -> `batching`); a window that waits, from the oldest queued chunk, as long as a drain costs (the verbatim: "a latency that adapts to match the time scaling of the umap updates"), skipped for a hover's chunks (`prioritize`). Live, four pages streamed through a hidden cell per arm, queue settled first (`scripts/check_batch_window.py`): window off -- 14 drains, **90.0** chunks a drain, wait p50 **70** / p95 448 ms; window on (cost 382 ms) -- 15 drains, **95.6** a drain, wait p50 **314** / p95 621 ms. +6% per drain, no drain saved, ~240 ms on every chunk: a page's chunks reach the queue in one burst from the embedder, so it is batched already. The window is therefore OFF by default (`ADAPTIVE_WINDOW`, `/plant adaptive-window`); the script re-measures it and says whether it earns its place (`window_helps`). An earlier run that put the window right after a restart's backlog read 9.6 s p95 and was discarded. `tests/test_batch_window.py`. |
| G-14 | **Graph linkage completeness** across trie, web links, markdown links | **Confirm** 2026-10-30 -- every axis in one live check (`checks/live.mjs` G-14), each leg on its own: store -> layout (did the sidecar hand the pane every link it holds?) and layout -> drawn (did the pane draw what it was handed?), over links whose BOTH pages are drawn. Web links **409 / 409 / 409** (stored / laid / drawn); markdown links **2 / 2 / 2** -- from a note the check writes and indexes, because the store held **0** linked vault notes and an empty axis cannot fail; code links **268 / 268 / 268**; the trie's directory edges **136 of 136** drawn. Across the store, 677 of 677 page links with both ends in the layout are carried, and the layout carries nothing the store lacks. Planted `plantTreeOnlyPages` (the one-parent rule over page links, run on the pane's own `treeOnly` over the edges it was handed): **51 of 474** web links kept -- caught. The trie inside an opened page is `G-6`'s. Was: **Partly — and the web-link axis is EMPTY for a measured reason, not an unbuilt one.** The trie axis is drawn and confirmed live (`G-6`). The web axis holds **2** `LinksTo` edges over 96 pages, and `store.relink()` — a repair that re-resolves every url address against the pages held now, since a link is otherwise resolved once at ingest against whatever existed then — adds **0**. The ceiling is 0, and here is why: of **29,186** url-valued addresses, **2,675** are anchored to a chunk and **72** resolve to a page this corpus holds, and the two sets **do not intersect**. The links that point at pages we have are in addresses no chunk draws; the links chunks draw point outside the corpus. That is a property of a collection of unrelated pages rather than of a crawl, and `relink` stays because on a crawl it is the difference between a link graph and a scatter **2026-10-24:** the store holds 767 page links between stored pages and the page layout carries 914 edges (link + dir); the page view DREW 232 of them -- `treeOnly`'s one-parent rule was dropping every web link into a page but one. Confined to containment: 914 of 914 drawn (`G-7`). |

| G-18 | Points are **quaternions and the distance between them is the quaternion distance** — the geodesic angle on the unit 3-sphere, `2·arccos(\|⟨p,q⟩\|)`, absolute because `q` and `−q` are one rotation | **Built, measured, not default** — `domvec/obsidian/spherical.py`, §M.9. It keeps **0.19** of the encoder's 10-NN neighbourhoods against the Euclidean fit's **0.38**. **"Not default" understates it: see D-11** — at the default page level `?space=S3` is accepted and ignored, so the user's *"apply the updates throughout"* (21:23) is not met by the surface they actually open |

What keeps the row Partly is the verbatim it decomposes: Addendum III's *"we center our focus on the origin"* is said of the SPHERICAL/quaternion space (`G-17`/`G-18`), where every point is at radius 1 and the origin is the centre by construction -- built, not the default, and its S² vs S³ question is still open for the user. Was: **Partly — true by construction, not true of the picture.** `spherical.to_ball` does send the identity rotation to the origin. But nothing puts the DATA near it: measured on 96 pages, the centroid sits 94.0 from the origin against a mean radius of 94.9 — every page on one side — so the origin-centred camera frames an empty middle with the corpus as a blob off to one side, which is `G-5` failing in S³. `_spherical_init` now centres the warm start (D-12), which moves it without fixing it. The verbatim is *"we center our focus on the origin"*, and that is a claim about where the data goes, not only where the camera looks |

Settled, after a browse (`checks/live.mjs` G-21 now waits for the overlap count to stop falling): **415 overlapping pairs of 792 nodes, 405 involving a picture** -- revealed pictures are presented at a readable on-screen size, larger than the node's room. Was: **Confirm** 2026-10-11 (was Defect D-25: 54 of 97 url nodes overlapping on screen) — the check measured fit-space separation against a fit-space radius and passed. The billboard half is untested at the pixel level **Re-measured 2026-10-11 in WORLD space** at the application's own `drawnRadiusOf` (the screen count conflates depth, `D-30`): 30 overlapping url-node pairs, every one a copy of a fixture page (`busy_feed.html` from two test ports) at the SAME fit point -- identical content, and pinned at rest the collider never parted them. Url-node targets are now relaxed like a page's chunks, spheres at the safety factor, collisions only (`graph.ts`, `roomStats.parted`; planted `plantPagesTogether`; `tests/camera.test.mjs`): **0 of 255** overlapping; planted **129**, worst coincident. | G-22 | **The chunking window counts only what a model reads.** Link targets — `href`, `src`, bare urls — are excluded from the token budget; the render keeps them | **Done** — `tokens.for_model`/`tokens.budget`, §M.7. 52.5% of the corpus's rendered tokens were addresses. **No live check, and deliberately so**: the comparison that matters is `budget(x)` against `count(x)` over the same string, and the tokenizer is server-side — a whitespace word count is not a baseline for a subword tokenizer, and a check built on one reported a link-heavy chunk at ratio 1.06 and called the exclusion broken. §M.7 is the measurement |
| G-23 | **Two vectors, two strings.** The quantized semantic embedding is given link-free markdown; the frequency matrix keeps url path words as lexical terms | **Done** — `vectors.VectorQueue.push` strips; `vectors.tokens` does not. An address is not a sentence; a url tail *is* a term |
| G-24 | **Exact duplicates are resolved in the database, over the aggregate DOM chunks, before any embedding is consulted** — page-scoped, byte-exact, no threshold | **Done** — `graphstore._same_content`, §M.8. Marked, not merged: `Anchors {ord}` pairs lines to addresses by position, so collapsing the nodes would misattribute every line |
| G-25 | **One node per distinct rendering in the projector**, with the copy count carried, not one node per address | **Done** — `layout.py`. archive.org drew a cluster of **125** identical nodes; dropped before the fit, because a duplicate embeds to the same vector and spends the projector's neighbourhood budget on a point it already has |

| G-28 | **The window is a staging device, not a hard constraint.** Stage one cuts conservatively at a window that is a strict subset of the display context; stage two merges back up | **Done** — stage one cuts at 256 link-free tokens, stage two merges up to `MERGE_WINDOW` 512. The 256 is deliberately half of it, so a merge of two neighbours fits by construction |
| G-29 | **"Exact duplicate" and "should be merged" are different verdicts and are decided separately.** Substring matching is explicitly rejected for the second | **Done** — two verdicts, two mechanisms: `G-24` decides exact duplicates on bytes with no threshold, and stage two decides merges on a radius read off the page's own neighbour distribution. Substring matching is used for neither |
| G-30 | **Frequency vectors verify the merge, as their own step, after dedup** — not mixed into the first pass | **Done 2026-09-19** — `workspace._lexically_verify`, called from `aggregate` after the semantic decision. Measured over the corpus: **19 pages, 748 groups, 14 flagged (1.9%)** — worst `ourworldindata.org/grapher/population` at 7 of 16. The merge STANDS and the doubt is recorded: tf-idf is the wrong instrument to overrule a meaning judgement with, and a page's furniture shares words with everything on it. Restricted to each group's own nonzero columns, which is exact for cosine and avoids densifying a corpus-wide vocabulary. `tests/test_aggregate.py` covers both arms plus the wiring |
| G-31 | **Aggregation is localised to a shadow DOM, not run over the corpus.** It is a second stage of *chunking*, inside the scan, coarse-to-fine — not a separate offline pass | **Done** — `aggregate_page` is called per url and sees one page at a time. The code stays in the chunk a reader opens and in the trie that addresses it. **Off by default for everything else**, deliberately: a web page's code sample is part of what that page says, and dropping it would change every existing embedding to serve a requirement about source files. A fit over the page's own chunks keeps **0.507**. The page's mean cannot stand in for its chunks, because the mean is exactly the direction they all share and what distinguishes them is what is left after it is removed. Global position comes from the page node, which already carries it; colour stays the global fit's, so two chunks on different pages that mean alike still look alike |

Two causes fixed in `markdown.py`: a heading before a record set was flushed alone (27 of the 43 -- NASA's "Highlights" over its cards, Python's `<h3>` over `<dl>` entries, RFC sections) and now goes with the first record within the bound; and the page-wide post-passes grew chunks after the descent sized them (`_tabulate` naming every row's columns, D-9: 26 of the 33 one Wikipedia comparison table), now re-cut by lines under the same lenient rule (`_recut`). A third cause fixed: a content boundary right after a lone heading flushed it alone (Python's `### Functions` over a 218-token `<dl>`, RFC 9110's sections) -- a buffer of headings only is carried whole (`PLANT_LONE_HEADING_FLUSH`). Re-measured: **22** heading-alone (before arm 43). The 22 head nothing (Wikidata "(0 entries)", an empty "Languages", archive.org's "Javascript is required" page, Slashdot ad slots) or sit over one indivisible line past 512 (httpbin 827, PostgreSQL 643), which the user's rule keeps apart. |

| G-89 | **The pending DIFF is drawn force-directed; what the transform has placed collapses into the page-node view.** Every chunk a drain produces is drawn at once as a force-directed node hung from its page -- built in the renderer from the drain itself (the sensor's MutationObserver chunking: no sidecar, no embedding, no UMAP) -- and stays so until that drain's chunks have been through the projector's transform; then its force nodes collapse into the page node (the default view draws pages, `G-53`; a page's inside opens on demand, `G-84`). Before any fit that is every scan; after one it is only the diff still waiting in the vectorization and transform queues. The fit is over page centroids and refits asynchronously (`G-83`); transforms run on the diffs, O(delta). **Phase contracts** (checked in order, in the running plugin, off `ui().graph` and the fresh-store run's phase trace): **C1 empty** -- a fresh store before any scan: 0 nodes, `layout_mode: "empty"`. **C2 first scan** -- the page's first drain draws at once: `layout_mode: "force"`, a page root plus one node per sensor chunk, each chunk with exactly ONE parent (nearest enclosing chunk by address, else the page); no vector waited for. **C3 delta** -- each drain adds exactly the chunks it emitted and removes exactly the ones it named removed; every other node keeps its identity and position; per-drain renderer time p95 < 50 ms. **C4 pages** -- each new page is its own tree. **C5 no fit yet** -- while the projector has no fit, the force trees stay up and the caption says it is waiting for the fit. **C6 the fit lands** -- the pane draws the page-node view from the projector; force nodes whose chunks are still unplaced stay drawn, hung from their page's projected node. **C7 collapse** -- when a drain's chunks have all been placed by the transform, its force nodes go (collapsed into the page node): pending force nodes fall to 0 once the queues are empty. **C8 after the fit** -- a new drain on a page the fit already holds adds force nodes under that page's projected node, and they collapse once transformed; a refit does not drop pending force nodes. **Interactions:** hover outlines and rings (`G-79`); a pin opens its billboard and survives the redraws (`G-78`); every node is on screen (`G-5`). **Planted:** the drain not wired to the graph must fail C2 | *2026-09-26: “the first handful of scans are rendered with a full force-directed layout as a fallback”*; | G-218 | **The store is compact: efficient storage, measured against what it holds; performance optimised throughout; no write lost in the switch, the original kept** *(2026-10-17, PLUGIN_GOAL 146)* | *Addendum X* | **Confirm** 2026-10-18 live (`scripts/compact_store.py`): the store rebuilt by kuzu's own EXPORT/IMPORT round trip -- **67.77 GB -> 1.40 GB**, every table's count identical (Page 922, Chunk 74,554, Address 1,791,913, Anchors 709,699, Contains 63,020, HasChunk 74,902, LinksTo 603, Layout 25), the export 0.37 GB; dropped only 2,548 `Anchors` and 10 `Contains` edges whose source chunk had been DELETED (an untyped `count(r)` counted them; a typed match with a key on each endpoint does not -- the first two runs aborted on exactly that and swapped nothing). Sidecar down ~30 s (stopped gracefully: supervisor first, `/shutdown` under the work lock; restarted with its own command line); the original kept as `graph.kuzu.orig-20261018-011143` for the reader to remove. Measured after: `select` 23-94 ms (was 0.6-1.4 s quiet, up to 140 s under load), capture lock waits max 2.3-2.7 s, median 0 (were 44-90 s). The plugin now waits out an unreachable sidecar however long it takes (a fetch failure had read as a refusal and DROPPED the drain); a refused one keeps 15 minutes. **2026-10-18 05:42, a second rebuild after a store defect**: from 04:08 the sidecar died natively in kuzu ~50 times in an hour (`crashes.log`: 0xC0000005 on the anchors/address writes; three write-ahead logs set aside; the supervisor restarting into the same replayed capture each time), so the store took no writes for ~1.5 h. Reproduced OFFLINE on a copy: inserting NEW `Address` rows segfaulted for some keys -- a plain `UNWIND ... CREATE (:Address)` of 400 rows, no edges -- while edges to existing addresses always passed: the Address primary-key index was damaged (**cause found later, 06:23-12:50**: the loop came back 15 minutes after the rebuild, and with every statement of the replayed capture dumped (`DOMVEC_DUMP_ROWS=1`) and replayed in a server on a copy: `COPY Anchors FROM df` -- 4d50da4's bulk anchor path -- was REFUSED by kuzu, "Buffer manager exception: Unable to allocate memory! The buffer pool is full", 41 rows on a 5.1 GB store with a 2 GiB pool, because appending to the populated rel table needs memory that grows with the table; and reads in flight on the second connection at that moment (`_warm_index`, `_warm_containment`) segfaulted. Earlier I had cleared COPY on a wrong reading; it was the trigger. Fixed: COPY for anchors is opt-in (`DOMVEC_ANCHOR_COPY=1`); a buffer-pool refusal poisons the store -- no further statement runs on it -- and `_poison_watch` restarts the process (75). `tests/test_store_poison.py` (4: poisoned vs planted `DOMVEC_PLANT_NO_POISON`, which runs the next statement). On a copy with the plugin's real queued captures: COPY on -> refused -> ACCESS VIOLATION in 35 s; default -> 366 statements, 234 anchor writes, no refusal, no crash, stopped gracefully). Remedy proven on the copy first: EXPORT/IMPORT, then the same 400-key insert, the 400-row fused statement and a 400-row COPY all pass where the original crashed. Applied with `compact_store.py`: 5.42 GB -> 1.44 GB, every table identical (Address 2,023,088, Anchors 864,561, Chunk 83,974, Contains 72,282, HasChunk 84,322, Page 1,056), 5 orphan Anchors dropped and reported; original kept as `graph.kuzu.orig-20261018-054234`. **And the markdown, a third time**: `integrity.log` shows chunk markdown EMPTY for 180 chunks at the 2026-10-17 22:36 start and **67,040 of 74,401** at the 00:44 start -- lost across that restart with nothing flagged, because the record's baseline was in memory and every loss so far (09-23, 10-07, 10-18) happened across a restart; every annas-archive homepage chunk drew an empty panel. Refilled by `POST /restore_markdown` from the JSONL checkpoint, by cid, only where empty: **66,449 restored in 32 s, 591 not in any checkpoint** (the 06:08 start record: `empty_md` 591). Now the record reads the previous PROCESS's last line from disk, and a rise of >= 500 empty heals at once (restore, then the lexical rows re-derived): `tests/test_integrity_log.py` -- a loss across a restart flagged and healed to 0; planted `DOMVEC_PLANT_NO_MD_HEAL` flagged and left empty **2026-10-24: a restart after the idle compaction STALLED** -- the child sat 10 min in the native preload (torch and both OpenBLAS loaded, numba not; 2.3 s of CPU; main thread in a kernel wait; RAM 12 GB free, disk idle) and the reader had no sidecar. Now: the child dumps every thread's Python stack to `stacks.log` each minute until its preload returns (`__main__.py`, faulthandler's C timer), so the next one names the import; and the supervisor kills a start with no `/health` in 240 s and no CPU for 60 s and starts another, logged "start stalled" (`supervise._run_child`, STUCK_CODE 70; planted `DOMVEC_PLANT_NO_START_DEADLINE`; `tests/test_supervise.py` 2 new). Cause of the hang itself not yet named. **Then, 23:23-23:36: five children in a row of one supervisor hung at interpreter startup** -- 13 MB, one thread, 0.02 s CPU, inside the Store Python's package lookup (`windows.staterepositorycore.dll`), before Python ran a line; the stall guard killed each at 243 s. A Python started from anywhere else came up in 0.1 s, and a fresh supervisor's first child came up at once: it is THAT supervisor's state after it compacted in-process. Kuzu alone does not reproduce it (a Store Python that built, exported and imported a kuzu db then started 3/3 children in < 0.1 s). Compaction now runs in its own process (`supervise._compact_in_child`, `python -m domvec.obsidian.compact`; planted `DOMVEC_PLANT_COMPACT_IN_PROCESS`); whether that ends the hang is for the next idle compaction to show in `crashes.log`. **It did not (2026-10-25 03:07-03:31):** a supervisor running the out-of-process code had two more starts stall, then its COMPACTION child hung the same way (13 MB, 0.016 s CPU) under a one-hour timeout, and the next child too -- so in-process kuzu was not the cause. Not reproduced in isolation: a Python started from the shell, a parent that terminated a sleeping child or a CUDA child, and a parent that ran kuzu all started children in < 0.1 s; every process in the tree is in a job object, so that does not separate them either. A fresh supervisor's first child always starts. What holds the sidecar down is therefore bounded instead: a child with < 0.5 s of CPU after 25 s never got past its start and is killed then (not at 4 min), and the compaction child is watched the same way -- stalled twice, the compaction is skipped and the sidecar starts (`supervise._run_child` early stall, `_compact_in_child`; `tests/test_supervise.py` 10). Cause still unnamed. **2026-10-25 04:01, two more bounds, both seen live.** The supervisor itself was heavy: `domvec.obsidian/__init__` imported `export` -> `graphstore`, so the parent of every stalled child held numpy, scipy and an OpenBLAS pool (536 modules, 53 threads, 71 MB). The package exports are lazy now and `__main__` imports the store only past `supervise`: 100 modules, no numpy/scipy/kuzu; live, 33 MB and 7 threads. And a supervisor whose starts stall `GIVE_UP_STALLS`=3 times writes the give-up marker (supervisor_gave_up.json, from `domvec/obsidian/supervise.py`) and exits; the plugin's 30 s `watchSidecar` reads the launch line `/health` advertises (cached in settings) and starts a fresh supervisor -- the one state known always to start its first child. Live, both arms: planted `__domvecPlantNoRespawn`, marker written and the sidecar stopped -> 2 watch ticks, 0 respawns, marker kept, sidecar down at 45 s; plant cleared -> marker consumed at ~25 s, a new supervisor (pid 44940, spawned by Obsidian), `/health` up ~50 s later. **Cause named and fixed (2026-10-25 04:50, `08ca088`).** Every stalled start was a child of a supervisor the MCP server launched, and inherited that server's stdin: the MCP stdio pipe, on which it sits in a blocking read between requests. On Windows a synchronous query on a pipe waits behind a pending read on it, so the child's interpreter startup waited for the next MCP message -- and the first child started only because the server was busy answering `sidecar_start`. Isolated: stdin a pipe another thread is blocked reading -> HUNG > 15 s; the same pipe unread 0.09 s; DEVNULL 0.08 s. Every child now gets NUL (`supervise._run_child`, planted `DOMVEC_PLANT_INHERIT_STDIN=1`; `tests/test_supervise.py` reproduces the mechanism, both arms), as do `start_sidecar` and the MCP's own runs. Live: an MCP-launched supervisor whose first child was killed started the second at once (CPU at 2 s, `/health` at 29 s); before, 6 of 6 such starts and 2 of 2 compaction children stalled. The Obsidian-spawned supervisor never had the pipe, which is why its compaction restart was clean (4,988 -> 1,871 MB, 04:35). |

| G-245 | **A url node stands at the centroid of its chunks** (`G-106`) *(2026-10-18, PLUGIN_GOAL 174)* | *Addendum XI* | **Partly.** Measured as the url node's distance from its drawn chunks' centroid over the cloud's rms radius. The off case is a page NEW to the store, while it is browsed (`scripts/check_stream_centroid.py`): two causes found. (1) The telescope equalises radii by rank, so a lopsided raw fit drew its mass to one side -- the url node at the edge of its own fan, 0.59-0.71 -- now the whole cloud is shifted onto the url node after the relax (`plantNoRecentre`); (2) chunks sat 118-193 units from their targets, calm-pinned, for 25 s+ -- now any chunk drawn more than max(2, 5% of its bound) from its target glides there (`plantTeleByForce`). At 20-35 s after landing: clean 0.11-0.33 (Splay tree, Skip list) vs planted (both) 0.53-1.23 (Treap, Scapegoat tree). (3) A glide that lands where its target no longer is now glides on (`stepGlides`). Second run, three new pages per arm, 20-35 s: clean 0.06-0.40 (median ~0.14), planted 0.02-1.13 (Rope 0.82-1.13). **Not yet**: the drawn chunks of a just-landed page still wander 75-110 units from centred targets at times; the source is not pinned. Caveat on every drawn-position number here: these probes ran with the Obsidian window COVERED, which Chromium throttles to ~1 frame a second (measured 2026-10-18); glides are time-based and unaffected, but the force simulation advances per frame, so force-driven settling runs ~60x slower than for a reader in front of it |

Live, on the reader's discourse topic, 3 cells: 6 hovers, 6 panels, **6 the hovered element's unit with the hovering cell's own lines**, 0 skipped; planted `__domvecPlantLastBrowser` (the old wiring): 6 panels, **0 right**. Hover -> panel p50 95 ms, max 126 ms (was p50 874, max 1,510 ms on a grey page). The 2026-10-18 measurements above ran with one cell, where the old wiring could not go wrong. In-app check `G-252` (a second cell opened behind the visible one on `result_cards.html`, 4 cells in all): **8/8** panels the hovered unit with the hovering cell's lines, 0 skipped; planted the old wiring **0/8** -- PASS. Capture latency is still 5-12 s a capture on a busy page; anchor writes are most of it (COPY is 33x faster per batch but stays opt-in, `graphstore._ANCHOR_COPY`) |

Built: every chunk carries `mdkey` (its exact markdown, hashed), a store-wide `_ContentIndex` keys one LEAD per content, and every other chunk with that key, on any page, is `dup_of` the lead -- the column the page-scoped rule (`_same_content`, `G-24`) already wrote, so drawing, embedding, `lead_vectors` and the panels follow unchanged. Marked, not merged: each chunk keeps its own node, anchors and addresses. After a refit the chunk view draws 59,922 (was 99,448). `/duplicates` reads the STORE: 0 plain contents drawn twice; 714 group leads share text with another drawn lead, kept by design. Not built: the PageRank over the TYPE graph weighting it. |

Not built: its nodes as an ego-UMAP network in the dialect. |

Found on the way: the craft (and the bisectors, the drawn plan) stayed in the OLD scene after the graph view rebuilt its scene -- drawn nowhere, unhittable; they rejoin the current scene each frame now. |

| D-53 | **DEFECT, fixed: the store's compaction died on every attempt since G-279, and the sidecar restarted each minute the reader was away** -- the hand-kept primary-key map in `compact.py` did not know the `Run` table G-279 added (2026-10-29), so counting `RunInput` asked for `a.rowid` ("Binder exception: Cannot find property rowid"), the compaction exited 1, and the supervisor started the sidecar again; the plugin asked again a minute later *(found 2026-10-30 in `crashes.log`: "compaction exited 1" every minute from 05:52, the sidecar unreachable between)* | **Confirm** 2026-10-30 live: the first idle-time compaction after the fix (06:47:23, `crashes.log`) compacted the working store **11,745.8 MB -> 1,878.2 MB**, swapped in (export 12.6 s, import 17.3 s) -- the store had grown uncompacted to nearly 12 GB since G-279. Fixed: `compact._pk` reads each table's primary key from the schema (`table_info`), the hand-kept map only a fallback. Tests: `tests/test_autocompact.py::test_a_store_with_runs_compacts_its_run_inputs_too` (Run + RunInput compacted, counts kept) and `::test_planted_hand_kept_pk_breaks_on_an_unknown_table` (the old lookup raises on `rowid`). |
| G-189 | **Chunk view stays responsive at 26k nodes** *(2026-10-12: "significant lag experienced here", right after G-188)* | **Confirm** 2026-10-12 live (chunk view, 26,846 nodes, 90 frames): p50 **20 ms**, p90 36, max 95 -- it was p50 211, p90 5,452, max 6,836. CPU profile: 53% in url view's open-page pass (`fitChunkSizes` -> `telescope` -> `relaxCloud`), which ran in chunk view over every chunk, synchronously, on every projector update and absorb -- now skipped there (planted `plantFitInChunkView`); and a second look pass over every node on every sync (`fitProxies`) -- now at most once a second. |

### P. The panels

**P-W — the per-chunk web panel**

| id | requirement | status |
|---|---|---|
| P-W1 | Specialized for the **interactive components** of webpages | Partial |
| P-W2 | The **native, editable** Obsidian markdown editor as one half | **Confirm** 2026-10-25 -- see `D-3`: the note's own body, in Obsidian's editor, is the markdown half; live check `D-3` |
| P-W3 | The interactive HTML card as the other, one per chunk, boundary set by the sensor | Done |
| P-W4 | A **minimal form of the original browser** — not raw HTML, not a browser re-render; same content, structure and affordances | Partial |
| P-W5 | Interactable **against the database, offline** | Partial |
| P-W6 | **And** dispatched to the live page when online | Partial — `enact` exists, gated off |
| P-W7 | **Media in both halves** | Partial |
| P-W8 | A switch between the two halves | Done |

**P-C — the code artifact panel**

| id | requirement | status |
|---|---|---|
| P-C1 | Full code artifacts **viewable and editable standalone** | **Confirm** 2026-11-01 -- live check `P-C1` (run 12, every arm; `obsidian-plugin/src/codeview.ts`, `GET`/`POST /source`): a module of this repository (`code://repo/...`, a scratch one the check makes and removes) opens standalone in the code artifact panel -- its FILE on the source side (a CodeMirror editor), its chunks as the store holds them on the trie side (4) -- and an edit typed into the editor, saved (`save` or Ctrl/Cmd+S), is on disk and re-indexed (1 chunk queued for a vector, 2 skipped). An installed package's module (`code://pypi/json/__init__.py`) opens read-only, no save. Planted `source-not-written`: the save leaves the file as it was. Opened from any code node's billboard (`code`). |
| P-C2 | **A terminal always directly below** the artifact — a kernel *run-and-output surface*, not a shell prompt (P-N2 §4a) | **Confirm** 2026-11-01 -- live check `P-C2` (run 12, both arms): the run-and-output surface sits DIRECTLY below the artifact's two sides (sides end at 935, the surface starts at 935) across their full width (44-428 both); `run` executes the module in a kernel of its own (keyed by its url, `__package__` set so a relative import resolves), and `alpha(21)` typed below answered **42** from that namespace. Planted `plantOtherKernel`: the expression met another kernel -- `NameError: name 'beta' is not defined`. A kernel surface, not a shell prompt (NOTEBOOK_PANEL_DESIGN 4a); the command terminal stays its own pane (`P-N2`). |
| P-C3 | **Artifacts vectorized by their comments and docstrings** | **Done 2026-09-20** — `G-52`. Measured over this repository: 985,336 rendered tokens → 304,039 for the encoder, 66.2% is code and is dropped |
| P-C4 | **Docstrings and code docs more broadly are the vectorized surface for codebases** | **Done 2026-09-20** — `G-52` again, and it reaches past Python: `G-50` gives JavaScript its comments (1,000 over `sensor.js`'s 2,870 lines) without pretending to parse it |
| P-C5 | **Live delta updates to modules** in both halves and in the 3D view | **Confirm** 2026-11-01 -- live check `P-C5` (run 12, every arm): a saved edit to one function's docstring is a delta in BOTH sides and the 3D view -- the save re-indexed the module (1 queued, 3 skipped by the embed gate, P-N6), the trie side re-read the store and marked the 2 chunks under the edited function and not its neighbour's, and those chunks reached the projector's delta stream (`/projector/delta`, what the 3D pane draws from) in 267 ms. Planted `plantNoTrieRefresh`: the trie side kept the old text; planted `source-not-projected` (dropped from the encoder and the projection queue): 0 of 2 in the stream over 20 s. One save took 11.0 s waiting on the work lock behind the encoder -- the measured case of `G-276`/`D-51`. |

**P-N — the retrieval-enhanced notebook panel**

| id | requirement | status |
|---|---|---|
| P-N1 | A **Jupyter kernel** panel, retrieval-enhanced | **Confirm** 2026-11-01 -- a REAL Jupyter kernel (PLUGIN_GOAL item 49: "Yes, install them" -- `jupyter_client` 8.10, `ipykernel` 7.4, `pyzmq` 27.2 installed with the reader's leave). `domvec/obsidian/kernel.py` `_JupyterKernel` drives the native `python3` kernelspec over the Jupyter messaging protocol behind the same per-note pool (one kernel a note, timeouts kill and restart, the reply unchanged: stdout, stderr, the trailing `text/plain`, the traceback, the execution count); the subprocess kernel remains the fallback when Jupyter is absent or will not start. Live check `P-N1` (run 6, both arms): run with the real Run control, a cell saw `ZMQInteractiveShell`, `%time` ran, state carried to the next cell (`state 499500`), and `search()` (2 hits) and `Vault` (`domvec-checks`) answered from inside it -- retrieval-enhanced. Planted `subprocess-kernel`: no IPython, the magic a SyntaxError. |
| P-N2 | **Switchable** between Jupyter kernels and PowerShell terminals. Resolved §4a: a *kernel run in place*, a *command terminal* in a **separate pane** | **Partial** — shells switchable from discovery (`GET /interpreters`) and running per pane; Jupyter kernels are now DRIVEN, not only listed (`P-N1`, 2026-11-01), but only the one installed kernelspec (`python3`) -- choosing another per cell is not built |
| P-N3 | A **toggle on a code block** opens terminal or kernel — possibly both at once. Under P-N2's resolution "both" is the panel running its kernel *while* a separate pane holds a terminal: two panes, no special layout | **Confirm** 2026-10-29 -- live check `P-N3` (`checks/live.mjs`, both arms): every runnable fence carries `▶ run` and `⌨ terminal` in live preview and reading view (`runbutton.ts`); `⌨ terminal` on a PowerShell cell opens a NEW terminal pane where the cell's command ran and printed (`TerminalView.runCell`, the shell its language names); `▶ run` on a Python cell then stored 42 in place while that pane stayed open -- both at once, two panes. Found on the way: a cell sent before the pane had listed the machine's shells went out with no shell, and the 400 that came back read as "no sidecar" (now waits for discovery; a non-JSON answer is named). Planted `__domvecPlantNoTermFence`: no terminal button |
| P-N4 | A facade over Obsidian's vault interface exposing local kernels to the filestructure | **Confirm** 2026-11-01 -- live check `P-N4` (run 21, both arms; `kernel_objects.vault_boot`, the plugin sends its vault folder with every run): a notebook in its own folder, run with the real Run control -- the cell started IN that folder (`cwd pn4 lab ...`), read the `data.csv` beside its note by bare name, and `Vault.write('made by a cell.md', ...)` made a note Obsidian's vault then held. `Vault`: `here`, `ls`, `read`, `write`, `glob`; reads anywhere in the vault, writes only inside it and never into `.obsidian` / `.git` (`tests/test_vault_facade.py`, 5). Planted `no-vault`: the cell started in the repository root and met `FileNotFoundError: data.csv`. Found on the way: a kernel left parked in its note's folder made that folder undeletable on Windows (EBUSY) -- the kernel now returns to the vault root after each cell, and the check's own cleanup deleting the folder is the proof. |
| P-N5 | Notebooks are **dynamically recursively chunked** like webpages | **Confirm** 2026-11-01 -- a note and its stored cell outputs chunk through the same recursive descent a page does, and now DYNAMICALLY: a notebook re-chunks on every save (live check `P-N6`: 15 chunks for five cells and an output, one changed chunk per one-cell edit). Outputs are addressed `.../output[n]` and retrievable. |
| P-N6 | …and **continuously diff-updated to the db**, re-vectorized and re-assigned to chunks | **Confirm** 2026-11-01 -- live check `P-N6` (run 5, every arm): a five-cell notebook, run once with the real Run control, is 15 chunks in the store; saving an edit to ONE cell re-indexed the note by itself (vault modify, no command) and queued **1** chunk for a vector, **14** skipped by the embed gate. After a restart (the gate's memory of the note dropped, `POST /vectors`) the same: **1** queued, **14** skipped -- the gate re-learns from the store's own embedded markdown (`VectorQueue.seed`, called by `ingest_chunks` only when a cid is unknown to it). Before this, a cid being keyed on POSITION and the gate on memory, the first edit after any restart re-embedded the whole note. Planted `no-seed`: **15** queued after the restart; planted `reembed-all`: **15** on every edit. (In both planted arms 14 of the 15 were answered from the in-process vector cache, which the simulated restart does not empty and a real one does: `queued` is the measure.) `tests/test_embed_seed.py` (4). Indexed on save: a note that has RUN (a stored readout) or is a slate -- a design doc's illustrative python block is not a notebook (`isReservoirNote`). |
| P-N7 | **One interface** centralizing retrieval and code research | **Confirm** 2026-11-01 -- live check `P-N7` (run 22, both arms), read as ONE notebook doing all of the verbatim's sentence ("centralize retrieval and code research into one interface where our chat contexts are localized to a panel, we can pull existing chunk panels into a new chat ... and run code experiments"): `search('radix tree')` run in a cell returned 3 database hits (Wikipedia's Radix tree first); `json.detect_encoding` sent from the 3D view's billboard became a prototype cell (`G-105`) that ran; the same billboard's new `chat` control pulled the chunk panel into THIS note's chat, and the next turn carried its cid `7785e0a60e460692` ahead of the rewound cell, the pin consumed -- another notebook's chat never saw it (chats are per note, `L-2`). Planted `plantDropPins`: only the cell was sent. Built: `NotebookChat.pin`, `main.pullToChat` (the notebook the reader was last in), the billboard `chat` control. |
| P-N8 | **Chunk scanning is exposed in the panel** — the reader can see the chunk boundaries the sensor drew | **Done** — a CM6 gutter marks every chunk's extent with its token count and flags any over the window, computed live by `POST /chunk_preview` through the same pipeline indexing uses. Editor only; reading view has no gutter |
| P-N9 | **Selection highlighting over the rendered markdown**, in the alternative panel | **Confirm** 2026-11-01 -- live check `P-N9` (run 8, every arm; `obsidian-plugin/src/marks.ts`): in READING view (the rendered side, NOTEBOOK_PANEL_DESIGN 5), a selection inside the Gamma section marked its chunk as an address -- `(/section/section[3], part 0)` under the note's `vault://` url, read from `/chunk_preview`, the store's own chunker -- and drew only that block highlighted; a section added ABOVE it (its ordinal becoming `section[4]`) left the mark on Gamma; selecting it again unmarked it. Planted `plantAddressOnly` (the ordinal address alone): the Delta mark slid onto its neighbour when a section was added above; planted `plantNoMarks`: nothing marked. A mark keeps the trie's two keys (CLAUDE.md 5b): the content hash finds a chunk that MOVED, the address one whose own text was edited. Seen: the screenshot `build_analytics/app_20261101_122402.png` -- the marked heading and paragraph tinted with an accent rule, the chat strip reading `context: 1 marked`. Marks persist in the plugin's data. |
| P-N10 | That selection **annotates the context fed to the SLM** — the reader marks what the model is given, rather than the model taking the whole panel | **Confirm** 2026-11-01 -- live check `P-N10` (run 9, both arms): with one chunk marked in a notebook, the chat's turn (`GET /chat`) was given exactly **one** context item, cid `2650c34f7c2ee842` -- the store's own chunk for the marked section (`sha1(url|xpath|part)`, checked against the `Chunk` row, whose markdown holds the marked text) -- resolved against the note's current text at send time; Hermes answered ("ok"). Planted `plantIgnoreMarks`: the rewind's cell (`cell:ab7aa98e`) was sent instead. The marks REPLACE the rewind while there are any; `clear marks` on the chat strip returns it to the rewind. A note with marks gets the chat even without a code cell. |

| id | requirement | status |
|---|---|---|

Planted `__domvecPlantNoTermFence`: the terminal option is gone from that same native view. What each option DOES is `P-N3` and `P-M4`, re-run green beside it (run 13). |
| P-M4 | Buttons that add new structural cells sit at the **very bottom** of the markdown | **Confirm** 2026-10-25 -- live check `P-M4` (`checks/live.mjs`, both arms; `obsidian-plugin/src/cellbar.ts`). One strip after the note's last line in live preview (below CodeMirror's content) and in reading view (nothing after it but the footer): `+ code` appends a python fence with the cursor in it, `+ terminal` opens a command terminal in its own pane (§4a), `+ browser cell` shows the browser cell. The new cell typed and run at once, with the reader typing in the prose meanwhile, kept its output (42). Read as (mine, recorded since the design doc left it open): L-2 keeps the chat at the selected cell's foot while that foot is IN the scroll window; everywhere else -- the cell scrolled away, the reading view, a marked note with no cells -- the scroll window gives up a 40 px band at the pane's foot and the collapsed strip rests in it, BELOW the boundary, never over text. Measured: foot in view -> strip at 649-677 inside a scroller ending at 979; scrolled to the end -> strip at 985-1013, below the scroller's 979, the note's last line clear above it; a marked note in reading view -> the same band (985 vs 979). Planted `plantNoDock`: the scroller runs to 1019 and the strip lies over it (985). L-2's own check re-ran green beside it (run 10). |

**P-B — the browser notebook cell**

| id | requirement | status |
|---|---|---|
| P-B1 | A fully-fledged **regular browser view** in the cell — NOT constrained to a chunk | **Done — seen live 2026-09-20** (`confirm.mjs`). An Electron `<webview>` with an address bar, back, forward and reload, navigating anywhere. Measured: loaded `books.toscrape.com`, followed a link, and went back |
| P-B2 | **Navigate exactly as in a normal browser**, while scanning continues on the live page | **Done — seen live 2026-09-20** (`confirm.mjs`, `P-B1`'s check). Navigation and back both work, and `live_capture_on` was TRUE throughout — the scanning loop stays armed while the reader browses, which is the half of this row that could have been quietly false. `G-16` confirms the chunks reach the projector |
| P-B3 | Embed the native **Selenium session view** in the cell if possible | **Superseded, not dropped** — the question this asked was answered the other way: the cell hosts an Electron browser and the SENSOR moved into it (`G-47`, verified against the scanner it replaces: 176 addresses, 14 chunks, 7 passes, identical). There is no Selenium window left to embed, which is the outcome Addendum II's "straddle" anticipated |
| P-B4 | **Resizing the cell resizes the Selenium window** | **Superseded** with `P-B3` — a `<webview>` flows with the DOM, so it resizes with the panel by construction rather than by driving a second process's window. That property is why the webview was chosen over `BrowserView` |
| P-B5 | The passive MutationObserver sensor fires throughout — scroll, hover, interact — diffing DOM → DB in O(delta) ms | **Partly** 2026-10-24, measured live by checks that run in the plugin: the fold is O(delta) in milliseconds on a shadow fixture (`S-1`); its phases travel with every drain and an unchanged merged run costs no lines (`G-66`: emit 2.0 ms vs 5.0 planted); pushed, not polled, through scroll and hover on discourse /latest (`D-40`: 10-11 of 10-11 new topics answered, hover's own share p95 15-51 ms in good runs) and through a navigation while the first capture stores (`D-35` PASS: page changes p95 128-134 ms, hover p95 17.5-31.6 ms). NOT yet: the store side in milliseconds -- a 23-topic capture's settle holds the store 5-20 s (`D-35`, `G-218`), and page -> host on a heavy feed is p95 100-165 ms against `D-40`'s 100 ms bar (the fold walk, ~35 us a node). |
| P-B6 | **Left click behaves exactly as in Firefox under Selenium** | **Done — seen live 2026-09-20** (`confirm.mjs`). Dispatched as a real input event to the guest rather than by setting `location`: the claim is that clicking behaves like clicking, and asserting it any other way would test the assignment instead. Measured: the click landed on `a-light-in-the-attic`, which is where that link goes |
| P-B7 | **Hover** overlays the chunk structure on the page's own cartesian layout, showing the chunks embedded in it | Confirm — `obsidian-plugin/src/chunkstructure.ts` (`embedded`): the hovered chunk's addresses share a prefix, its patricia node; that node's parent is the container it is embedded in, and the other chunks under it are its structure (up to `MAX_UP` 3 levels when the parent holds no other, never the page itself; a chunk whose node holds the hovered one is its frame, not a sibling). `PipelineBox.placeStructure` outlines them faintly in the cell above the page, one read of the page for all of them, nothing written into it; re-measured on scroll; gone on leave. `tests/chunkstructure.test.mjs`. Live (`confirm.mjs P-B7`, 2026-10-11, `shadow_catalogue.html`): hovering result card 3 gave the container `/html/body/result-list/#shadow-root/div`, 4 sibling cards, **4 of 4 measured inside the container's own box on the page** (read independently through the sensor), the hovered card not among them, 1 on screen and drawn (3 below the fold), none after leaving. Planted `plantNoStructure`: the hover outline only. **It found a hover defect on the way**: the sensor's push named only its in-page chunk, which can be COARSER than the store's (the whole shadow-DOM list is one in-page chunk), and every card resolved to the page header's stored chunk -- the outline, the camera and the panel all went to the header. The push now carries the hovered element's own composed address (`sensor.js` `hoverAddress`, spelled as the fold spells it) and the host resolves it by the store's nearest-ancestor walk; planted `plantHoverById` reproduces the header. And a pointer that left the page and came back to the SAME element was never hovered again (`lastHoverTarget` survived the leave) |
| P-B8 | **Right click locally indexes a chunk to its dual-view markdown panel inside the cell** — this is what feeds the SLM | **Done — seen live 2026-09-19**, and it is `G-39` by another name: the webview's own `context-menu` event, `elementFromPoint` at the cursor, `GET /resolve` walking UP to the nearest chunk the trie holds, and Obsidian's own `MarkdownRenderer` in the panel's other face. Measured: 2 ancestors up to an `article`, 199 chars over 10 rendered elements |
| P-B9 | Right-click indexing covers the **static type set only**; interactive components are deferred to tool calls in much later iterations | By design |
| P-B10 | **Dual per-chunk browser + markdown panels are reachable from the projector too** | **Done — seen live 2026-09-19**. Both halves: `G-40` takes the browser to the clicked chunk (parked at scrollY 4945, click moved it to 363) and `G-57` opens the markdown as a billboard beside the node (19 lines, anchored to 0px, follows the camera, pins on a drag) |
| P-B11 | **Straddle the shipped core `webviewer` and take ownership of Electron** as the work proceeds — use it, and outgrow it | **Done** — the cell is a `<webview>` chosen over `BrowserView`/`WebContentsView` because it flows with the DOM and therefore resizes with the panel, and the scanning that used to need Selenium now runs in it (`G-47`) |

### R. Retrieval — the `[]()` gesture

| id | requirement | status |
|---|---|---|
| R-1 | Typing the four symbols makes the bracket contents a **semantic retrieval query** | Confirm — `obsidian-plugin/src/gesture.ts` (`gestureIn`): `[query](` with the paired `)` after the cursor, or `[query]()` before it; three characters or more, never an image's `![alt]()`, never a filled link; `tests/gesture.test.mjs`. The query goes to the workspace's one ranked list (`/search`). live `confirm.mjs R-3` 2026-10-11, in a native note: `[patricia trie]()` with the cursor between the parentheses opened 9 ranked results, 17 after scrolling to the list's foot |
| R-2 | Results in **scrollable whitespace immediately below the line** | Confirm — `linkgesture.ts`: a block under the gesture's line, its list scrolling at 220 px and asking for the next page of the ranking as it nears its foot (`G-69`'s offset); a scroll during a slow page is kept and answered when the page lands (dropped, the list sat at its first page: the first search after a plugin reload took 20 s+ behind the projector), and the list fills itself until it can scroll. The end is the server's own `more`, never a page that did not arrive. live `confirm.mjs R-3` 2026-10-11, in a native note: `[patricia trie]()` with the cursor between the parentheses opened 9 ranked results, 17 after scrolling to the list's foot; scrollable before the click |
| R-3 | The remaining editable lines stay **uninterrupted** — the panel is one cohesive unit. `EditorSuggest` overlays; a CM6 **block widget** reflows | Confirm — built as the design note settled it: a CodeMirror 6 block widget, from a state-computed decoration (CM6 refuses blocks from a view plugin), its DOM owned by one controller so results appended in place keep the reader's scroll; the editor reflows around it. Measured live (`confirm.mjs R-3`, 2026-10-11): the line below moved down **250 px for a 250 px block**, not covered. Planted `plantOverlay` -- the floating `EditorSuggest` shape: the line below moved **0 px** and the results covered it |
| R-4 | Clicking a result **infills the link between the parentheses** | Confirm — the click writes the hit's destination inside the `()` and closes the block: a vault note by its path, a page with an exported note by that note (a markdown-panel link), anything else by its own address (`main.ts` `gesture.destination`, `VaultLinks.noteFor`). Live (`confirm.mjs R-3`, 2026-10-11): the line became `[patricia trie](https://duckduckgo.com/?q=patricia+trie&ia=web)` -- the top hit is a stored search-results page, which is the ranking's answer, not the gesture's; 1 infill, block closed. The exported-note branch is not yet seen live |
| R-5 | **All retrieval comes through this one interface** — web, vault, packages alike | **Partly** — the gesture asks the one `/search` ranking, which holds web pages, vault notes (`vault://`) and source files (`code://`) together; it adds no index of its own. **Not yet ONE interface**: the older `/` gesture (`retrieval.ts`, passages inset with rules) and the projector's retrieval overlay (`G-69`) are retrieval surfaces beside it. Whether they stay is the reader's ruling (*"all retrieval comes from the singular `[]()` interface"*), not removed here |
| R-6 | Package retrieval applies a **vector-centroid pre-filter**, then partition-wise retrieval; the filter must not become a second interface | **Confirm** 2026-10-12 live, real embeddings, 5 code partitions: each query keeps 3 and the package it is about is always among them -- "decode a JSON document" keeps repo/json/urllib (hits json, repo); "HTTP client status codes" urllib/http/html (hits http, urllib); "parse a URL" repo/urllib/html (hits repo, urllib); "escape html entities" urllib/http/html (hits html, http). Built inside the one `/search` ranking (`Search._prefilter_code`): each code root is a partition with a unit-mean centroid; a query keeps the 3 nearest and ranks code only within them; web and vault rows never masked; `SearchDiagnostics.code_partitions` reports it. `tests/test_code_prefilter.py` (3, planted `code_partitions = 0`). |
| R-7 | The docs are updated to match this gesture wherever they disagree | Done — 2026-10-11, a search of every doc for the gesture: `docs/OBSIDIAN_BRIDGE.md` named the `/` gesture as THE retrieval gesture -- it now names `[]()` (`gesture.ts`, `linkgesture.ts`) and marks `/` as the older surface awaiting the `R-5` ruling; `docs/NOTEBOOK_PANEL_DESIGN.md`'s sketch drew `[[]]` (Obsidian's wikilink) as the gesture, now `[query]()`. `PLUGIN_GOAL.md`'s design note already described the built shape |

### L. The minimal embedded chat

Deliberately small. Everything below is the whole of it; the chained
single-hop architecture from the first statement is **not** in scope here.

| id | requirement | status |
|---|---|---|
| L-1 | A **quantized** small model — Nous Hermes 2 DPO 7B on local CUDA | **Confirm** 2026-10-30 -- `Nous-Hermes-2-Mistral-7B-DPO.Q4_0.gguf` was already installed in the GPT4All model directory (no download); loaded on **CUDA** in 2.9 s (`domvec/chat.py` `HermesBackend`, n_ctx 8192, falls back to CPU only when the GPU has no room), and it answers the browser chat live (`G-43`: 7.3 s for a 3,223-character turn, 0.5 s for a follow-up). |
| L-2 | Embedded **within the notebook panels**, at the bottom of the selected cell — markdown, code, or terminal/kernel | **Confirm** 2026-10-30 -- built (`obsidian-plugin/src/notebookchat.ts`, `rewind.ts`, through `POST /chat`). live (`checks/live.mjs` L-2, a notebook note written to the vault, opened in the editor, the model L-1's): the panel appears on a note holding a code cell and sits **at the foot of the selected cell** (within 6 px of the cell's last line); markdown and code cells alike (it landed on a code cell with its output, then on a paragraph); seen on screen (`build_analytics/app_20261030_155150.png`). |
| L-3 | Context **begins at the last-most viewable cell in the current selection** and **rewinds backwards** through the notebook | **Confirm** 2026-10-30 -- the rewind's terminal point is the cell at the END of the selection (the last-most cell in it) and it goes backwards, oldest first; nothing after the terminal cell is sent. live (`checks/live.mjs` L-2, a notebook note written to the vault, opened in the editor, the model L-1's): cursor on the paragraph after a failing code cell, `@2` sent exactly those two cells. `tests/rewind.test.mjs` (6). |
| L-4 | It rewinds over the notebook **in its current state**, including terminal and kernel output — which is the norm for intermediate cells | **Confirm** 2026-10-30 -- a code cell rewinds WITH the output block the kernel wrote under it (`<!-- nb-output -->`), as the note holds it now; a changed output is a new cell (its hash moves). Live: the reply named the output's error -- "no 'sightings.csv' file found ... ensure that the 'sightings.csv' file exists". |
| L-5 | The prompt/response panel **hovers and follows the scroll** | **Confirm** 2026-10-30 -- the panel hovers: it opens on hover, and as the note scrolls it is clamped inside the view (after a 900 px scroll it was still fully on screen) and returns to the cell's foot when the cell is in view. |
| L-6 | A **new rewind only when the cursor lands on a new cell**, not on scroll | **Confirm** 2026-10-30 -- a rewind is applied when the cursor lands on a NEW cell: moving within the same cell (from its code into its output) applied none, a 900 px scroll applied none, landing on the next cell applied one. Planted `plantRewindOnScroll`: **2** rewinds from two scrolls -- caught. |
| L-7 | The rewind is **injected into the user prompt** as the effective contextual history | **Confirm** 2026-10-30 -- the rewound cells go ahead of the prompt in one message (`domvec/chat.py` `message`), the conversation's effective history: turn 1 was 452 characters, two cells and the question. |
| L-8 | The **user prompt decides how many further chunks** to rewind | **Confirm** 2026-10-30 -- `@N` in the prompt is how many cells to rewind (taken out of the prompt; none means as many as fit): `@2` rewound 2. |
| L-9 | Rewind size stays within a **safety factor of the max input token limit**, so sessions continue without compaction or one-shot restart | **Confirm** 2026-10-30 -- one rewind is at most **35%** of the model's 8,192-token window (11,468 characters at 4 a token), so a session goes on without compaction or a one-shot restart; and a cell already in the conversation is not sent again (turn 2, same two cells: **0** sent, **2** dropped, 39 characters). Live, from the last paragraph of a 44-cell note: **18** cells, 11,106 characters, under the budget. Planted `plantNoBudget`: all 44 cells, 25,002 characters -- caught. |
| L-10 | Also available **hovering over the native markdown panels** | **Partly** 2026-10-30 -- over the browser cell's native markdown face the chat is the browser chat's strip (`G-43`, in the page area, over either face), and in a note it works in the editor (live preview and source). A note in READING view has no cursor to land a rewind on, and is not covered. |
| L-11 | Deferred by name: cell/code navigation, running things in the terminal, writing updates back to notebooks | Deferred |

### X. How the work is done

| id | requirement | status |
|---|---|---|
| X-1 | **Search GitHub for working codebases and examples at every step** — *"the most important drivers of success"* — so the code the model uses to touch the interface is visible | Standing |
| X-2 | Verify in the running app over devtools, **never by screenshot** | Standing |
| X-3 | Where a document contradicts the goal, the **document** changes | Standing |
| X-4 | Measure the distribution **before** writing an acceptance criterion | Standing — four criteria here were written first and all four were wrong |
| X-5 | Cleanups gate on **`accounted`**, not `covered` | Standing |

**X-6 — the build order is given, and it is not the order of these tables.**

The addendum states it as a sequence, so it is a requirement and not a
suggestion: the **native Obsidian markdown panel is the substrate**, and
everything is grown from it — *"we essentially build the native obsidian
markdown out into this more fully fledged interactive panel"*.

1. native markdown panel, kept in native form (P-M1)
2. + the same retrieval (P-M2)
3. + code, kernel and terminal options (P-M3, P-N)
4. + the hovering chat (L-10)
5. + **interactive web browser cells — explicitly "the final step"** (P-B)

P-M and P-N are therefore not peer panel types: P-N is P-M grown, and the
native form survives alongside it. P-B is last because *"the way browser
interactions and dual panel views work is slightly different, and particularly
so."*

**X-7 — Addendum II refines that order for the near term.** It does not
replace X-6; the notebook panel still grows from the native substrate and
browser cells still come last.

1. **Re-cut the corpus at 256** — done first so nothing downstream is measured
   against a window the goal no longer names.
2. **Verify the duplication capabilities** — §M.1 and §M.2 above; *"at least
   verify the capabilities of to explore"*.
3. **Notebook kernel panel design, nailed down first** — *"we move onward to
   the notebook kernel panel design first to nail it down correctly"*.
4. **Bring the Electron sensor in when ready** — the phrase is *"when ready"*,
   so S-13's verdict gates this rather than a date.
5. **Then the hovering SLM bar** — *"then, we go for the hovering SLM bar
   itself and work out the details"*.

Note what moved: the hovering chat was item 4 of X-6 and is item 5 here,
behind the notebook panel. Nothing was dropped.

---

## Part II — measured findings

Everything below is evidence for Part I, kept under the `R-*` ids it was
recorded against. Those ids no longer number requirements; they anchor
measurements. Where Part I cites a number, it comes from here.

## A. The panel — the artifact view

| # | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| R-A1 | "interactive interface that is slim with the markdown-centric chunks with interactives themselves" | Done |
| R-A2 | "distilled DOM rendered as interactive obsidian object structures … as shown by the rendered and interactive fields where 'properties' is" | Done |
| R-A3 | "we don't need 'properties' at the very top of each panel … remove this top panel" | Done |
| R-A4 | "at the very top of each panel, we can have a simple switch between the markdown and the shadow DOM button" | Done |
| R-A5 | The panel occupies the top of the note *(implied by R-A3: the panel takes the place properties vacated)* | Done |
| R-A6 | "the whitespace could be a little tighter to border the content" | Done |
| R-A7 | "there is quite a bit that is duplicated/exploded" — a chunk draws each thing once | Done |
| R-A8 | "remove the duplicate prose below the panel" | Done |
| R-A9 | "Images, interactive fields rendered properly" | Done |

**R-A2** renders into a shadow root carrying its own stylesheet, written in
Obsidian's own custom properties so a control follows the reader's theme.
Obsidian's native property rows are the visual reference.

**R-A3** is the vault setting Obsidian's own Appearance pane writes
(`propertiesInDocument: hidden`), not a plugin reaching around it. The
frontmatter stays — `cid` is what the graph, the link map and the chunk pane
resolve on — it is only not drawn.

**R-A4** shows *both* states with the live one marked. A single button
labelled with the other state is ambiguous exactly where it matters: it can
equally mean "you are looking at markdown" and "press for markdown".

**R-A6** verified 813px → 183px on one chunk, old spacing against new on the
same content.

**R-A7** verified live: 5 lines, 5 distinct, nothing repeated. A product card
had been drawing its title five times — the image's alt, the picture's
caption, the wrapping div's visible text, the anchor's `title`, and the
heading. All five are real addresses; markdown must spend a line on each
because a line is all markdown has, and HTML need not.

**R-A8** folds the prose into a collapsed callout rather than deleting it. A
callout is a blockquote, so the wikilinks in it are still parsed into the
graph, Obsidian's search still finds the words, and the file stays a
plain-text archive. Deleting outright would cost every vault edge unless the
links moved to frontmatter.

---

## B. Two forms, one distillation

| # | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| R-B1 | "markdown-centric chunks … **or** a more pure html extension to render original web content directly but still in distilled, organized, and slim minimal-html chunks, where we can keep the interactive and the static/media asset components" | Done — both |
| R-B2 | "Switch between read-only interactive web elements and markdown distillation renderings" | Done |
| R-B3 | "aligned to the same graph RAG database with triple retrieval" | Done |
| R-B4 | "a simple button or hotkey" | Done |

**R-B1** was offered as an either/or and both were built. The markdown form
*reconstructs* widgets from a rendering; the HTML form emits an `<input>`
because the trie says the element is a control. Both are built from **one
payload** — the same line-to-address alignment used twice — so a chunk cannot
say one thing in one form and something else in the other.

**R-B4** ships the switch and a command. No default hotkey: Obsidian asks
plugins not to claim one, and a conflict is silent.

---

## C. Structural fidelity — the weakest area

| # | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| R-C1 | "If there is a possible way to retain as much as the original web interface while keeping the distilled html something that could be rendered, that would be a big deal" | **Partial** — reach 35.2% |
| R-C2 | "the structures like boxes and nested things can stay and be reconstructed from the patricia trie path nodes perhaps if we stick to absolute xpaths" | **Partial** — reading order 76% |
| R-C3 | "matching cartesian coordinates kind of thing on the page (as in we keep all our structures distilled, but localized enough still such that we can render them as their intended cohesive structures)" | **Partial** — see the constraint below |
| R-C4 | "switch to a more generalized xpath structure that keeps absolute structure with agnostic array indices for multiple/repeat fields like menu items, paragraphs, search results" | Done — as a second field, not a replacement |
| R-C5 | "chunks should be at their context window limits and measured using markdown renderings for token counts" | **Partial** — one chunk is 15x the window |
| R-C6 | "Our chunking should be such that, for example, we get search results as singular chunks" | Done |
| R-C7 | "…and semantic + frequency vectors" | Done |

### Acceptance criteria for the partials

**R-C1 — reach ≥ 60% mean.** Today 35.2%. Some of the gap is deliberate
(dedup, suppressed taxonomies, hidden elements) and some is loss; nothing yet
separates the two, so the first task is to classify the unreached elements
before trying to reach them. A reach number that rises by un-suppressing noise
would be worse than the one it replaced.

**R-C2 — reading order ≥ 95%.** Today 76%: a quarter of chunks draw their
elements in an order the page does not present them in. Reading order is the
one structural property a reader notices immediately.

**R-C3 — a stated constraint, not a shortfall to close.** The sensor captures
**no box geometry, by design** (`tests/test_no_box_geometry.py` enforces it),
so literal cartesian coordinates are not available and cannot be without
changing the sensor's contract. What stands in for them is *containment*: the
distilled tree's nesting is the page's grouping, and the record rule (R-C6)
makes each group a chunk, so a chunk arrives already localised to one cohesive
thing. A group holding one picture with words beside it is drawn as a card.
**Accepting containment-as-geometry, or changing the sensor to capture boxes,
is a decision for the user — not one to settle by implementation.**

**R-C5 — the window is not enforced on a single oversized VALUE.**

Reported by the user, who opened the note; the board had it as MET. The
metric was `chunks_over_window`, which counts how MANY chunks exceed the
window and never by how much, so 1.2% read as met while one chunk sat at
**7,745 tokens against a window of 512 — fifteen times over**. The board now
carries `worst_overflow_ratio` beside it, with a bar of 1.5x, so this cannot
hide again.

It was read first as a chunking problem -- one value too big to cut -- and an
offset-splitter was written for it twice, and reverted twice, because the
worst chunk did not move. The user's own reading was the correct one:
**under-chunking**. Not a value that resists division, a value that should
never have existed.

data.gov.uk's publisher filter is a `<select>` of 1,511 `<option>` elements.
The tagger names a control with `_own_label`, which walks every descendant --
right for `<button><span>Menu</span></button>`, wrong here, because a
listbox's descendants are its OPTIONS, and the tagger already emits each one
as its own `interactive.inputs` detection at its own xpath. So the option list
was recorded twice: once as 1,509 addressed lines, and once more as a single
43,542-character label on the select. The reader got a wall of text with one
address for 1,511 things, sitting beside the same content properly addressed.

The browser is the authority on what a collapsed select draws, and
`analytics/groundtruth.py` already wrote the rule down -- "a `<select>` draws
the one value it holds". `_chosen_option` makes the tagger agree: the option
carrying `selected`, and otherwise nothing. Falling back to the first option
was tried and rejected as a fabrication -- this filter opens on an empty
`<option value="">`, so the box a reader sees is blank, and naming it after
the first real entry would report a filter as applied when it is not. The
control still renders (`[ ] select`), and the `<label for=publisher>` beside
it still says `Publisher`.

Measured over the frozen corpus, in-process, with the change toggled on
identical input:

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| worst chunk | 7,745 tokens (**15.1x**) | 2,997 tokens (**5.9x**) |
| chunks | 22,504 | 22,500 |
| distinct addresses | 335,982 | 335,982 |
| covered addresses | 287,763 | 287,762 |
| `chunks_over_window` | 0.72% | 0.72% |

Four chunks disappear -- the four aggregates, on the corpus's four listboxes.
No address is lost from the trie.

**What remains, and why it is a different problem.** The new worst chunk is
Project Gutenberg's *Pride and Prejudice*, `/html/body/p[1099]` -- Darcy's
letter, one genuine paragraph of 2,997 tokens. That IS a single indivisible
unit, and it is the case the offset-splitter was really for. Two constraints
bind any such split and the reverted attempts held neither:

* It cannot consult the window. `test_no_address_is_lost_when_the_window_
  changes` holds that the covered address set is identical at every setting,
  so cutting at the window would mint window-dependent addresses -- 713 of
  them, measured.
* It must run where every path sees it, not inside `_emit_runs`.

The vocabulary is already there: `text()@412` is a documented accessor form
and `sensor.js` cuts long text into budget-sized pieces addressed by offset.
The offline path does not yet.

Acceptance: `worst_overflow_ratio` <= 1.5x with the covered address set
unchanged across windows.

**R-C4 — the measurement, and what it says.**

    addresses                   23,654
    distinct by shape            2,831   (88.0% fewer)
    shapes covering >1 element   1,290   median 5, p90 31, max 1511
    of those, holding DIFFERENT content
                                 1,022   (79.2%)

The premise holds: the corpus really is mostly repeated templates. The naive
form does not: four shapes in five cover elements that say different things,
and `/html/head/script` alone covers three unrelated script urls. An address
is a *pointer* — the REPL presses it, `enact` resolves it against a live DOM,
the store keys chunks on it — and a shape cannot point at either sibling.

**The design that follows:** keep the indexed absolute xpath as the identity,
add the shape as a **second, derived field** on `ContentAddress`. Additive, so
no key changes and no rows migrate — the same rule `render_class` already
follows. It buys grouping across repeats, retrieval by template ("every result
row"), and stability across re-scans, since an inserted sibling shifts every
index and no shape.

**What it will not fix:** of the 420 duplicate chunks, only 97 are the same
template on the same page. Most duplication in the projector is repeated
*content* at unrelated places, which wants content-hash dedup — a separate
change (R-E3a below).

---

## D. Graph and links

| # | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| R-D1 | "plugs into Obsidian graph view" | Done |
| R-D2 | "Align markdown graph view links with web links with web chunks" | Done |
| R-D3 | "Clickable markdown deciding which vault artifact to inline hyperlink via the panel's full patricia trie address" | Done |
| R-D4 | "Source the right other plugin packages first to see how they work, then fork findings over" | Done |

**R-D1** deliberately does not touch Obsidian's own graph view. It has no
plugin API, and the one community plugin that extends it patches undocumented
internals and warns in its own README that settings can be lost. Real
wikilinks in real notes make the native graph work for free.

**R-D4** produced the decision behind the shadow root: of Obsidian's
HTML-rendering plugins, those taking *arbitrary* markup all sandbox in an
iframe, because arbitrary markup carries scripts. `html-blocks` is the
exception and uses a shadow root per block, for style isolation. Ours is
neither arbitrary nor scripted, so the iframe's isolation buys nothing and
costs the two things that matter — event wiring to trie addresses, and
Obsidian's CSS variables reaching in.

---

## E. The 3D projector

| # | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| R-E1 | "equivalent-dimensional pre-UMAP concatenations of the frequency vectors to the same dimensionality as the semantic ones, and then a final-stage concatenated UMAP with equal information dimensions" | Done |
| R-E2 | "some HSV dimensions as well to the UMAP to make it 6D" | Done |
| R-E3 | "Image and media billboards for distilled HTML" / "images should show in the 3D viewer" | **Done — seen live 2026-09-19** — `G-15` (media present in the projector) and `G-21` (a billboard is COLLIDED as a panel, half-diagonal, not as a sphere — sizing them as spheres left 96 overlapping pairs) |
| R-E4 | "For each 3D node … when I click, I should only see the chunk itself as either structured markdown or interactive HTML" | Done |
| R-E5 | "3D projector has no viewable nodes" — the view must show its nodes | Done — **re-broken and re-fixed**, see R-J4 |

**R-E1** verified: `frequency + semantic · equal halves of 768 · equalised
true`, on real nomic vectors.

**R-E2** spends three of six components on hue, saturation and value from the
*same* fit. Colour was a hash of the host, which is a legend, not a
measurement. `fitted_dims` is reported beside `requested_dims` and the colour
is dropped when the fit could not produce it — asserted from the measured
width, never from the intention.

**R-E3 acceptance: ≥ 95% of nodes carrying a picture render it.** Today 74%.
The 66 failures are dead asset urls in a frozen corpus, not a code path — a
live re-scan is the test, not a fix.

**R-E5** was a real failure with two causes, both worth keeping in view: the
projection arrived off-origin at a scale that moved with the corpus, and
`zoomToFit` only adjusts distance along the current view direction, so a
camera inside the cloud is "fitted" from inside it. Measured: 0.32% of pixels
lit from inside, 5.56% from outside. The layout is delivered centred in a
known cube and the camera is *placed* before it is fitted; a **recentre**
button exists because a reader who orbits inside has no way back.

### Proposed, not yet requested

**R-E3a — content-hash dedup of chunks.** 13.4% of chunks are exact markdown
duplicates, and walking four hops from a real article lands on two distinct
nodes both reading "Games". This inflates the projector and the embedding
matrix. Flagged here because it was *observed*, not asked for; it needs the
user's call, since two identical chunks at different addresses are arguably
two real things.

---

## F. Retrieval and editing

| # | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| R-F1 | "a `/` gesture in the markdown panel rendering a Kindle/Kobo-style inline retrieval template" | Done |
| R-F2 | "Retrieval interlaced with content editing" | **Partial** |
